**Lemma 1 (Holomorphicity).**

The projection $\Pi_i := \mathrm{pr}_{\M_{g_i,n_i}}\circ\mathrm{pr}_2|_\mathcal S$ is holomorphic, and $\Pi_i = Y\circ p$ where $Y(t) := Y_t$. To see $Y$ is well-defined and holomorphic: for $t_0\in\Delta$, choose $q_0\in\partial V_{t_0}$ (nonempty since dim 1... need nonemptiness: $\dim\partial V_t = 1$ implies nonempty). Since $p$ is a holomorphic submersion, there is a simply connected neighborhood $D\ni t_0$ and a holomorphic section $\sigma:D\to\mathcal S$ with $\sigma(t_0) = q_0$. Then $Y|_D = \Pi_i\circ\sigma$ — wait: need $\Pi_i\circ\sigma$ to equal $Y$: $\sigma(t)\in\partial V_t$ so $\Pi_i(\sigma(t)) = Y_t = Y(t)$: YES since $\pi_i(\partial V_t) = \{Y_t\}$. So $Y|_D = \Pi_i\circ\sigma$: holomorphic. ✓. (Note: no well-definedness issue since $Y_t$ is by hypothesis the single point.)

Also record: $Y(0) = Y_0$; and $\partial V_t\subset\{Y_t\}\times\Delta'$ where $\Delta' := \prod_{j\ne i}\M_{g_j,n_j}$.

**Proposition 5.** Under the standing hypotheses, $Y_t = Y_0$ for all $t$.

**Proof.** By Lemmas 3 and 4 we may fix a maximal stratum $\Delta_{\Gamma^*}$, an open set $U$, rigid configurations $R_1,\dots,R_m$ such that (5.1): $\overline{V_t}\cap\Delta_{\Gamma^*}\cap U = \{(Y_t,R_a)\}$ for all $t$, each point being a cusp of $\partial V_t$; moreover, by Lemma 3(b), $\overline{V_t}\cap\Delta_{\Gamma^{**}}\cap U=\emptyset$ for every refinement $\Gamma^{**}$ of $\Gamma^*$ at vertex $i$.

Now: the deepest locus $\overline{V_t}\cap\Delta_{\Gamma^*}\cap U$ is, by hypothesis (i) [applied at $\Delta_{\Gamma^*}$], a smoothly trivial family over $\Delta$. Since the rigid coordinates are locally constant on $\Delta_{\Gamma^*}$, the trivialization yields holomorphic maps $\zeta_a:\Delta\to\M_{g_i,n_i}$ with $\overline{V_t}\cap\Delta_{\Gamma^*}\cap U = \{(\zeta_a(t), R_a)\}$, and by (5.1) and Lemma 1, $\zeta_a = Y$ for each $a$.

**The key point.** We claim the family $\{(\zeta_a(t),R_a)\}$ is CONSTANT in $t$. Granting the claim, $Y = \zeta_a \equiv Y_0$. To prove the claim, consider the prime decomposition (Theorem B) of the deepest locus at $t=0$: the finite set $\{(Y_0,R_a)\}$ decomposes into prime pieces, each a single point, and each prime piece projects — trivially, but canonically with respect to the stratification — to the factors of its ambient sub-product $\M_{g_i,n_i}\times\prod_{j\ne i}(\text{rigid moduli})$; the piece over the $i$-factor is the point $Y_0$. By the compatibility hypothesis (ii), the same decomposition, with the same ambient sub-products and the same combinatorial incidence with the chain of strata between $\Delta_\Gamma$ and $\Delta_{\Gamma^*}$, holds for every $t$: the deepest locus of $\overline{V_t}$ is glued to the higher strata exactly as at $t=0$. Since the non-$i$ coordinates of every prime piece are rigid and the family is smoothly trivial, each prime piece is a holomorphic arc $t\mapsto(Y_t, R_a)$ in the slice direction; the incidence data being constant, the only possible motion is in the $\M_{g_i,n_i}$-direction. **§1 Setup.**

Let $\overline{\M}_g = \M_g\sqcup\partial\M_g$ be the Deligne–Mumford compactification; boundary strata $\Delta_\Gamma$ indexed by stable dual graphs; per the convention of the problem, we only consider strata for which $\Delta_\Gamma\cong\prod_{j=1}^k\M_{g_j,n_j}$ (no finite quotient), with $\pi_j$ the projections. A subvariety of such a product is *totally geodesic* (in the sense of [arXiv:2412.06765, §2]; cf. [arXiv:2412.16330]) if a component of its preimage in $\prod_j\T_{g_j,n_j}$ is totally geodesic for the product Teichmüller metric; complex geodesics are the 1-dimensional case.

Standing hypotheses (H1) = (i), (H2) = (ii), plus: $V_0$ smooth, dim 2, totally geodesic; $\mathcal V\subset\M_g\times\Delta$ holomorphic family, smoothly trivial; $\partial V_t := \overline{V_t}\cap\Delta_\Gamma$; $\dim\partial V_t = 1$; $\exists i$: $\pi_i(\partial V_t) = \{Y_t\}$ for all $t$.

Main Theorem: $Y_t = Y_0\ \forall t$.
